- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - New Deal (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): New Deal: Landmarks and Great Person improvements produce +6 of their base yield types ( Manufactory/Citadel, Town, Academy, Embassy, Holy Site, Landmark), and +2 Tourism
      - Capitalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Capitalism: 2 Specialists in each of your cities generate +1 Happiness instead of -1 Unhappiness from Urbanization. Specialists generate +1 Gold and Science.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

GuardedBias and AfraidBias increased to 9 - must be extremely defensive and cautious. HostileBias increased to 8 to reflect our stance against Aztec/Denmark axis. Meanness slightly increased to 4 for more aggressive defensive demands. We are fighting for survival, not expansion.)
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Transnationalism (Policy)
        - Rationale: Transnationalism available in 4 turns. This Level 2 Freedom tenet is critical: 15% franchise appearance rate (3x normal), +25% global franchise max, Towns count as franchises, and +2 Culture from Corporation Offices. With International Finance Center built and Spaceflight Regulations imposing +25% gold purchase penalty on spaceship parts, I need massive gold generation. Transnationalism provides this through franchises.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Kyiv
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Removing Kyiv priority override (previously 100/95). We are at war with Kyiv due to Aztec alliance; we cannot influence them while at war. The Aztecs will likely win their Sphere of Influence proposal. Shifting resources to flippable city-states instead of wasting effort on an unreachable target during war.

We lost our +2 delegates from this former ally and must focus on protecting our remaining permanent allies (Prague and Vatican City).

        - Public: 100

        - UpdatedTurn: 412

        - UpdatedTurn: 412
        
        ### Panama City
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 50
        - Rationale: Raising Panama City relationship from 0/0 to 50/50. The spy mission quest against Montezuma (1,013 Science + 419 Influence) is worth nearly one full turn of our entire empire's science output. While Agent Baalhaan needs time to build network in Tenochtitlan (352/1494, +18/turn), signaling positive relationship ensures the AI prioritizes this mission once network is established. This quest could provide the crucial science boost needed to catch the Aztecs.
        - UpdatedTurn: 413

        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Japan declared war on us this turn after Aztecs liberated Satsuma to them. They are now part of the Aztec alliance system against us. Despite having zero military strength, they provide diplomatic cover for Aztec aggression. Setting permanent hostility to ensure AI treats them as an enemy combatant and doesn't waste resources on diplomatic overtures until war ends.
        - UpdatedTurn: 414

Public max maintained for voting coordination, private opinion slightly tempered to 90 to reflect ideological differences that may cause friction on future proposals.

        # Victory Progress
        Victory Progress: current progress towards each type of victory.
        
        ## DominationVictory

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Taoism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - New Deal (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): New Deal: Landmarks and Great Person improvements produce +6 of their base yield types ( Manufactory/Citadel, Town, Academy, Embassy, Holy Site, Landmark), and +2 Tourism
      - Capitalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Capitalism: 2 Specialists in each of your cities generate +1 Happiness instead of -1 Unhappiness from Urbanization. Specialists generate +1 Gold and Science.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Equality (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness in every City and +1 Happiness for every 15 Citizens in a City.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from and Distress, Poverty, Illiteracy, and Boredom in all Cities.
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Mobile Tactics
        - Rationale: Setting Next Research to Mobile Tactics. This technology unlocks Mechanized Infantry, which is a massive upgrade to our frontline defense (strength 80) and does not require strategic resources. It also leads to Stealth, which is crucial for late-game air dominance and survival against the Aztec war machine.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Transnationalism
        - Rationale: Confirming Transnationalism as our next policy. With our severe gold deficit (-252 GPT) and the need for massive gold generation to purchase spaceship parts (due to Spaceflight Regulations penalty), Transnationalism is our best choice. It turns our Towns into franchises and triples franchise appearance rate, boosting our gold income significantly.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Wonder: 0
          - Infrastructure: 40
          - Defense: 100
          - NavalRecon: 50
          - NavalTileImprovement: 40
          - UseNuke: 90
          - Happiness: 40
          - Recon: 5
          - Ranged: 90
          - Expansion: 0
          - AirCarrier: 75
          - Antiair: 100
          - Nuke: 100
          - Naval: 60
          - Culture: 10
          - Growth: 30
          - Production: 100
          - WaterConnection: 30
          - Espionage: 100
          - Airlift: 50
          - NavalGrowth: 40
          - Religion: 0
          - Mobilization: 100
          - CityDefense: 100
          - MilitaryTraining: 75
          - Air: 100
          - Mobile: 50
          - Science: 100
          - TileImprovement: 25
          - GreatPeople: 100
          - Gold: 100
          - Spaceship: 100
          - Offense: 30
          - Diplomacy: 50
        - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
      - rationale: Adjusting flavors to optimize defense and economy. Mobile flavor is raised to 50 to encourage the AI to train a Light Tank for the Vatican City quest (which will reward us with 191 Influence and 422 Gold) and to improve our defensive mobility. Air flavor is maxed at 100 to prioritize rebuilding our air force after losing a Fighter and a Heavy Bomber to Aztec intercepts. Production, Science, Gold, and Espionage remain at 100 to sustain our economy, complete Apollo in 5 turns, and secure spy mission science.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 52933
    - reasoningTokens: 9939
    - outputTokens: 458
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Opponent's Strength**
            
            I'm currently assessing Montezuma's formidable score of 9403, recognizing the significant challenge he presents. This high standing suggests a potent military or economic advantage I need to understand and potentially counter.
            
            **Evaluating Threats**
            
            I am focusing on Montezuma's immense military strength and science advantage. His domination victory condition is a critical concern, especially given our current state of war and his recent nuclear strike. I'm also beginning to analyze Denmark's position.
            
            **Assessing Alliance Status**
            
            My current focus is on our precarious alliance situation. While allied with Byzantium, facing their Happiness penalty and Denmark's invasion is concerning. Carthage itself is struggling, with cities under siege and experiencing severe happiness deficits, directly impacting combat effectiveness.
            
            **Analyzing Delegate Strength**
            
            My focus has shifted to the World Congress. While I observe the delegate counts, my immediate attention is drawn to the recent peace with Vancouver, a welcome development. I'm also tracking the progress of the Apollo Program and our bomber construction.
            
            **Developing Counter-Strategy**
            
            I'm analyzing the aggressive Aztec actions and their overwhelming force near Carthago Nova. Simultaneously, I'm preparing our defenses with Rocket Artilleries and Machine Guns. The current research focus is on Lasers, and I'm planning to adopt "Transnationalism" as our next policy.
            
            **Selecting Next Tech**
            
            My current thought process is focused on choosing our next technology. I've identified two primary options: Electronics and Mobile Tactics. I am currently weighing the benefits of each, specifically considering the units they unlock and their impact on our ongoing defensive efforts.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            My focus has shifted to selecting our next policy after setting research to Mobile Tactics. I'm reviewing the available Freedom Level 2 options, particularly considering how they might complement our existing strategies and address immediate concerns, and I recall Transnationalism was previously on the table.
            
            **Adopting Policy Transnationalism**
            
            I'm confirming Transnationalism as our next policy, set to adopt in five turns. This choice is strategic, aiming to significantly boost gold generation, especially with the International Finance Center built and Spaceflight Regulations in effect. I've also reviewed our spy network and a compelling quest opportunity against Montezuma.
            
            **Completing Spy Quests**
            
            I'm currently confirming the completion of our spy quest with Sokoto, as our influence suggests it may have already been resolved. My focus is now on initiating the spy mission against Montezuma from Panama City, ensuring Agent Baalhaan in Tenochtitlan remains to fulfill this objective. I'm also investigating other active quests, particularly concerning the Vatican.
            
            **Completing Spy Quests**
            
            I'm confirming the completion of our spy quest with Sokoto, as our influence suggests it may have already been resolved. My focus is now on initiating the spy mission against Montezuma from Panama City, ensuring Agent Baalhaan in Tenochtitlan remains to fulfill this objective. I'm also investigating other active quests, particularly concerning the Vatican.
            
            **Pursuing Site Opportunities**
            
            An archaeologist is almost ready near Hippo Regius. I'm pleased the AI will likely send it to the Vatican City site for a potential quest reward of Influence and Tourism. I'm also noting Vatican City's request for a high-XP Light Tank, though our current capabilities are limited.
            
            **Managing Diplomatic Relations**
            
            I'm re-evaluating my alliance with Prague, confused by our negative influence despite being permanent allies due to the "Carthaginian Sphere of Influence" resolution. Their request for tribute from Vancouver seems problematic given our current peace, but their ongoing war with Denmark and the Aztecs is a significant factor.
            
            **Navigating Congressional Threats**
            
            I'm analyzing the World Congress, particularly China's proposal to repeal our influence over Prague. This is a significant concern, as its passage would immediately sever our alliance. I'm trying to understand how a defeated civilization's proposal remains active and assessing the delegate situation.
            
            **Voting Against Repeal**
            
            I must vote against China's proposal to repeal our influence over Prague. Their bloc, allied with Denmark, holds the majority of delegates, posing a direct threat to our alliance. I'll prioritize this vote to maintain our diplomatic standing.
            
            **Clarifying Alliance Status**
            
            I'm clarifying the complex relationship with Prague. Despite being permanent allies, negative influence persists due to a past forced war declaration. While we share common enemies in the Aztecs and Denmark, I need to understand how to improve this influence given current wartime conditions and resolutions.
            
            **Defending Key Locations**
            
            I'm assessing the immediate threats to Prague and Carthago Nova. Prague needs support against the Aztecs, and Carthago Nova is critically low on health while building a crucial Heavy Bomber. I must decide how to best allocate our limited forces to defend both.
            
            **Reinforcing Key Defenses**
            
            My focus is on fortifying Carthago Nova. A new Fleet Destroyer has been trained there, and along with existing Battleships and a Mobile SAM, we have naval strength to consider. However, the city's defense is critical given the overwhelming Aztec threat and its high defense strength.
            
            **Analyzing Gold Deficit**
            
            I am deeply concerned about our current gold deficit. Despite significant gold generation from cities, our net per turn is a stark -252. I'm investigating unit maintenance costs, specifically due to the "Global Peace Accords" resolution, to understand this imbalance.
            
            **Establishing Trade Routes**
            
            I'm focusing on improving our gold situation by establishing more trade routes. We have ample capacity and can currently generate significant gold per turn by activating more routes. I will also explore selling surplus incense to Byzantium for immediate gains.
            
            **Securing Trade Gains**
            
            I'm confirming our advantageous trade deals with Byzantium, securing much-needed resources. With a focus on gold generation, I'm evaluating further trade opportunities and considering selling surplus luxuries. I'm also shifting my attention to our conflict with Denmark, noting their unhappiness and low military strength while confirming the razing of Satsuma.
            
            **Defending Critical Cities**
            
            I'm analyzing the Aztec assault on Carthago Nova, noting their impressive modern armor and air units. Our own defenses there are currently comprised of a Modern Armor unit, a Machine Gun, and the city's considerable defensive strength. I've also realized a fighter and heavy bomber were lost during the recent engagements.
            
            **Shifting Grand Strategy**
            
            The loss of two bombers is a significant setback for our air power, especially with Carthago Nova critically low on health. Given the dire defensive situation and the overwhelming Aztec threat, I'm now considering a radical shift away from the Spaceship grand strategy to prioritize immediate survival and defense.
            
            **Prioritizing Victory Path**
            
            Despite recent setbacks, I'm reaffirming our commitment to a science victory. While the Aztecs are technically ahead in techs and have completed Apollo, our own Apollo is nearing completion. Survival remains paramount, and science offers our only viable path to victory against their overwhelming military and cultural dominance.
            
            **Revisiting Diplomatic Ties**
            
            I am reviewing our diplomatic relationships, noting our strong alliances with Byzantium and our clear hostilities with the Aztecs and Denmark. I've also reconsidered Vancouver's status; despite a prior drop in influence, I believe maintaining a positive relationship with them remains beneficial for our long-term strategy.

        - #2:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: ApAL1PfpsRznXtgI
          - toolName: set-policy
          - input:
            - Policy: Transnationalism
            - Rationale: Confirming Transnationalism as our next policy. With our severe gold deficit (-252 GPT) and the need for massive gold generation to purchase spaceship parts (due to Spaceflight Regulations penalty), Transnationalism is our best choice. It turns our Towns into franchises and triples franchise appearance rate, boosting our gold income significantly.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: kBg8mJxhq0CuwkmV
          - toolName: set-flavors
          - input:
            - Flavors:
              - Wonder: 0

              - Diplomacy: 50
            - GrandStrategy: Spaceship
            - Rationale: Adjusting flavors to optimize defense and economy. Mobile flavor is raised to 50 to encourage the AI to train a Light Tank for the Vatican City quest (which will reward us with 191 Influence and 422 Gold) and to improve our defensive mobility.
